    "content": "Previous Candidates:\n[\"Generalized Anxiety Disorder\"]\n\nInterview Transcript:\n\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm visiting today because I've been struggling with severe sleep problems and constant, uncontrollable worry for almost two years now—about a year and ten months, to be exact. It feels like I just can't turn my mind off or get any decent rest. I really need some help figuring out how to manage this.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant changes in your mood, such as feelings of sadness, hopelessness, or loss of interest in activities you once enjoyed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really feel sad or hopeless, and I haven't lost interest in my usual activities. It's more that I feel constantly wound up and anxious, and my body is always so stiff and tense from all the worrying.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific situations, events, or topics that trigger or worsen your anxiety and worry, or is it more of a general, free-floating feeling?\n\nPatient: It's definitely more of a general, constant feeling rather than being triggered by one specific event. My worry seems to spread across almost every area of my life, including my work, family, and finances. It just feels like there is always something to be anxious about, no matter how well things are going.\n\nDoctor: How much do these worries and anxieties interfere with your daily life, relationships, and ability to function at work or in social situations?\n\nPatient: The worries constantly pop into my head, which makes it really hard to focus on my daily tasks and get things done. But I'm not quite sure how to measure or describe the overall impact it's having on my relationships or social life.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how these worries affect your ability to complete tasks, meet deadlines, or fulfill responsibilities at work or at home?\n\nPatient: My worries constantly intrude into my mind, making it very difficult to focus on whatever task I'm trying to do. This happens both at work and at home because my anxieties span across so many different areas of my life. It ultimately slows me down quite a bit and makes it really hard to meet my responsibilities and deadlines.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have trouble sustaining focus on tasks because you're worried about something, or do you find your mind wandering or having difficulty paying attention even when you're not actively worrying about anything?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's definitely because of the worries. They suddenly intrude into my mind and completely disrupt my concentration while I'm trying to get things done, rather than my mind just wandering on its own.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a typical day for you, including how your anxiety affects your morning routine, work or school, and leisure activities, to help me better understand the extent of its impact on your daily life?\n\nPatient: My day usually starts with me waking up feeling completely exhausted and unrefreshed, with my neck and shoulders incredibly stiff and tense. Throughout the day, I feel constantly wound up like a spring, and intrusive worries keep popping into my head, making it impossible to focus on work or relax during my free time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have difficulty paying attention or following through on instructions, even when you're not feeling anxious or worried, such as when reading a book, watching a movie, or engaging in a hobby?\n\nPatient: No, when I'm not feeling anxious or worried, I generally don't have any difficulty paying attention or following instructions. If my mind is actually calm, I can read a book or watch a movie without any issues.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that you've noticed helps to calm your worries or reduce your anxiety, even if it's just temporarily, or are there any patterns to when your anxiety tends to be better or worse throughout the day?\n\nPatient: I've tried to distract myself or force myself to stop worrying, but nothing really seems to help or calm me down. I also haven't noticed any specific patterns or times of day when it's better or worse, as the anxiety and tension feel pretty constant throughout the entire day."

========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{"candidates": ["Generalized Anxiety Disorder", "Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder (Predominantly Inattentive Presentation)"], "note": "The patient's symptoms of constant, uncontrollable worry, difficulty focusing due to intrusive worries, and feeling 'wound up' support the continued consideration of Generalized Anxiety Disorder. However, the patient's reports of difficulty sustaining focus on tasks and having trouble completing tasks due to intrusive worries also raise the possibility of Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder (Predominantly Inattentive Presentation), as the patient's symptoms of inattention could be contributing to their functional impairment. The fact that the patient can focus when not feeling anxious suggests that their attention difficulties may be related to their anxiety, but it does not rule out the possibility of an attentional disorder. Further evaluation is needed to determine the relative contributions of anxiety and attentional difficulties to the patient's symptoms.", "is_final": false}

It just feels like there is always something to be anxious about, no matter how well things are going.\n\nDoctor: How much do these worries and anxieties interfere with your daily life, relationships, and ability to function at work or in social situations?\n\nPatient: The worries constantly pop into my head, which makes it really hard to focus on my daily tasks and get things done. This happens both at work and at home because my anxieties span across so many different areas of my life. It ultimately slows me down quite a bit and makes it really hard to meet my responsibilities and deadlines.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have trouble sustaining focus on tasks because you're worried about something, or do you find your mind wandering or having difficulty paying attention even when you're not actively worrying about anything?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's definitely because of the worries. They suddenly intrude into my mind and completely disrupt my concentration while I'm trying to get things done, rather than my mind just wandering on its own.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a typical day for you, including how your anxiety affects your morning routine, work or school, and leisure activities, to help me better understand the extent of its impact on your daily life?\n\nPatient: My day usually starts with me waking up feeling completely exhausted and unrefreshed, with my neck and shoulders incredibly stiff and tense. Throughout the day, I feel constantly wound up like a spring, and intrusive worries keep popping into my head, making it impossible to focus on work or relax during my free time.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have difficulty paying attention or following through on instructions, even when you're not feeling anxious or worried, such as when reading a book, watching a movie, or engaging in a hobby?\n\nPatient: No, when I'm not feeling anxious or worried, I generally don't have any difficulty paying attention or following instructions.
